I came in because... well, my husband and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I'm crazy. But I'm not. I know they've been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. It's so obvious if you just look at the numbers. Speaking of numbers, did you know I used to be really good at math back in high school? I mean, I didn't finish, but I was always the one figuring out the tips at restaurants. We used to go to this diner on 4th Street, they had the best cherry pie. I haven't had cherry pie in ages. My grandmother used to bake it from scratch, you know, with the lattice top and everything. Well, I don't know about destiny, but I definitely have a role at the warehouse that they don't want to admit. Well, yeah, like the truck I was telling you about. It's all connected. Everyday things are definitely part of it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Broadcasted? Like... like on a radio? No, I don't know why you keep asking about my thoughts. Like I've been saying this whole time, I only came in because my husband and the warehouse crew are acting like I'm the crazy one. But I know they're the ones watching my moves and messing with the pallet counts to keep me out of that supervisor spot. It's not my thoughts, it's my job they're messing with. 

If I was supervisor, I'd get one of those heavy-duty walkie-talkies. The ones they give us on the floor now are basically toys. The batteries are always dying. I actually went to buy some double-As at the drugstore on Tuesday so I could just bring my own, but they were locked behind that thick plastic glass stuff. You have to press a little red button for an employee to come open it. I waited like ten minutes and nobody even came. The store was totally empty, too. Just me and this guy at the front buying a huge bag of dog food. It looked like the kind my aunt used to buy for her Golden Retriever. That dog shed everywhere. You couldn't sit on her living room couch without getting covered in blonde hair. She tried putting a blanket over it, but the dog would just pull it down... but anyway, no, my thoughts are my own. Nobody is broadcasting them.

They were the really comfortable kind, too, the bootcut ones. I used to wear them when I went line dancing with my sister. We'd go down to this place called The Rusty Spur on Friday nights. They had a mechanical bull, but I never rode it because I get motion sickness really easy. Even riding in the back seat of a car makes me dizzy. My mom used to have to give me Dramamine just to go to the grocery store when I was little... but anyway, no, nobody is taking my thoughts or anything. They're just watching my moves at the warehouse.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like everyday events, like something you see or hear, are specifically related to you or have a hidden meaning that only you understand?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, like the truck I was telling you about. It's all connected. Everyday things are definitely part of it. Like I keep saying, I only came in today because my husband and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I'm the one who's crazy. But I know what's going on. They're watching my moves, changing the pallet counts so I don't get that supervisor spot. It's so obvious to me. \n\nSpeaking of obvious, you know what's not obvious? How to fix a leaky faucet. My kitchen sink has been dripping for three weeks. Drip, drip, drip. It drives me up the wall when I'm trying to sleep. I tried to tighten the little knob thing under the sink, but I think I made it worse. I really should just call a plumber, but they charge an arm and a leg just to walk through the door. My neighbor, Gary, he said he'd look at it, but he's always busy working on his classic car in the driveway. It's a '67 Mustang, cherry red. Beautiful car. He spends every weekend waxing it. I used to want a convertible when I was a kid, so I could feel the wind in my hair... but anyway, yeah, the things I see at work, they definitely have a meaning that they think I don't see, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are not your own, or that your thoughts are being broadcasted for others to hear?\n\nPatient: Broadcasted? Like... like on a radio? No, I don't know why you keep asking about my thoughts. Like I've been saying this whole time, I only came in because my husband and the warehouse crew are acting like I'm the crazy one. But I know they're the ones watching my moves and messing with the pallet counts to keep me out of that supervisor spot. It's not my thoughts, it's my job they're messing with. \n\nIf I was supervisor, I'd get one of those heavy-duty walkie-talkies. The ones they give us on the floor now are basically toys. The batteries are always dying. I actually went to buy some double-As at the drugstore on Tuesday so I could just bring my own, but they were locked behind that thick plastic glass stuff. You have to press a little red button for an employee to come open it. I waited like ten minutes and nobody even came. The store was totally empty, too. Just me and this guy at the front buying a huge bag of dog food. It looked like the kind my aunt used to buy for her Golden Retriever. That dog shed everywhere. You couldn't sit on her living room couch without getting covered in blonde hair. She tried putting a blanket over it, but the dog would just pull it down... but anyway, no, my thoughts are my own. Nobody is broadcasting them.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever felt that certain things people say or do, even if not directed at you, actually have a special hidden meaning that only you understand?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, yeah, I guess so. I mean, they think they're being sneaky with the things they do, but I see right through it. Like I keep telling you, it's the whole reason I'm even sitting here. My husband and the people at the warehouse are treating me like I'm the one losing my mind, but I know exactly what they're doing. They're watching every single move I make on the floor, and they're deliberately changing the pallet counts just to sabotage me so I don't get that supervisor position. 

It's just like how they keep changing the schedule without telling me. The schedule is printed on this cheap paper, you know, the kind that jams the printer if you don't load it exactly right. We had a printer like that at my old job at the diner. It would always jam right when we needed to print the daily specials. The specials were usually meatloaf on Tuesdays and fried chicken on Thursdays. I always liked the fried chicken day because the cook, Hector, he made the best gravy. He used real bacon grease in it. It wasn't exactly healthy, but man, it tasted good. My doctor told me I need to watch my cholesterol, so I try not to eat stuff like that anymore. I've been trying to eat more oatmeal for breakfast, but it's just so bland unless you put a ton of brown sugar in it, which kind of defeats the purpose, I guess... but yeah, they think I don't know what they're doing with the pallets, but I do.

Well, I don't know about TV or songs, really. It's more about the things happening right in front of me. Like I've been saying, I'm only here because my husband and the warehouse crew are trying to make it look like I'm crazy. But I know what's really going on. Cartoons used to be so much better back then, you know? They actually had a storyline. Now it's just flashing lights and loud noises. It gives me a headache if I watch it for too long. I usually just take an aspirin and go lay down in a dark room when I get a headache like that. We used to go to this diner on 4th Street, they had the best cherry pie. I haven't had cherry pie in ages. My grandmother used to bake it from scratch, you know, with the lattice top and everything. My husband doesn't believe me, he just acts like I'm losing it, but I know what I'm seeing. I've always been really observant, you know? Like, I notice things other people just walk right past. They're trying to intimidate me so I don't go for that supervisor spot. They know I'm onto them changing the pallet counts. Speaking of trucks, my uncle used to drive a big rig. He'd haul freight all the way from Texas to Chicago. He brought me back a snow globe once from a truck stop in Missouri. It had a little arch inside it, and when you shook it, the snow was gold instead of white. I kept it on my dresser for years until my cat knocked it off and it shattered everywhere. Man, that cat was a menace. He was an orange tabby, always getting into things. But anyway, yeah, the red truck... it's all part of how my husband and the people at work are trying to make me look crazy, but I know what's really going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that you have a special role, destiny, or importance that others don't recognize, or that events happening around you are specifically directed at you in a way that others don't understand?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know about destiny, but I definitely have a role at the warehouse that they don't want to admit. Like I was saying, my husband and the people at work, they just keep acting like I'm crazy. But I know for a fact they've been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts so I don't get that supervisor spot. It's all connected. Just like how everything is connected if you really pay attention. I used to watch this show on TV about detectives connecting the dots on these big boards with red string. I always thought I'd be good at that, you know? Being a detective. My cousin actually wanted to be a cop, but he failed the physical because he has flat feet. He ended up working at a hardware store instead, mixing paint. I love the smell of fresh paint. We painted my bedroom a light blue when I was a teenager, like a sky blue. We got the paint on sale... but yeah, they're definitely targeting me with those pallet numbers because they know I'm the only one who actually understands how the warehouse should be run.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, taken out of your mind, or that others can hear what you're thinking?\n\nPatient: Put thoughts in my head? No, I don't... they don't need to do that. They don't need to hear what I'm thinking because they're already watching everything I do. Like I keep telling everyone, that's the whole reason I even came in here today. My husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm crazy, but I know they have been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. They're just doing it right out in the open on the floor. \n\nWe use these big wooden pallets mostly, the ones that are always splintering on the edges. You have to be careful or they'll rip right through your jeans. I ruined my favorite pair of Levi's that way last year. Got them caught on a stray nail on a busted pallet. They were the really comfortable kind, too, the bootcut ones. I used to wear them when I went line dancing with my sister. We'd go down to this place called The Rusty Spur on Friday nights. They had a mechanical bull, but I never rode it because I get motion sickness really easy. Even riding in the back seat of a car makes me dizzy. My mom used to have to give me Dramamine just to go to the grocery store when I was little... but anyway, no, nobody is taking my thoughts or anything. They're just watching my moves at the warehouse.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like everyday events, like something you see or hear, are specifically related to you or have a hidden meaning that only you understand?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, like the truck I was telling you about. My neighbor, Gary, he said he'd look at it, but he's always busy working on his classic car in the driveway. It's a '67 Mustang, cherry red. Beautiful car. He spends every weekend waxing it. I used to want a convertible when I was a kid, so I could feel the wind in my hair... but anyway, yeah, the things I see at work, they definitely have a meaning that they think I don't see, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are not your own, or that your thoughts are being broadcasted for others to hear?\n\nPatient: Broadcasted? I always liked the fried chicken day because the cook, Hector, he made the best gravy. He used real bacon grease in it. It wasn't exactly healthy, but man, it tasted good. My doctor told me I need to watch my cholesterol, so I try not to eat stuff like that anymore. I've been trying to eat more oatmeal for breakfast, but it's just so bland unless you put a ton of brown sugar in it, which kind of defeats the purpose, I guess... but yeah, they think I don't know what they're doing with the pallets, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you sometimes feel that random or ordinary things — like something on TV, a song playing in the background, or something someone says in passing — are actually sending you a message or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know about TV or songs, really. I love those little twisty pretzels, especially the ones with the extra salt. I used to buy the huge tubs of them at the wholesale club when my kids were little. They would eat them by the handful while watching cartoons on Saturday mornings. Cartoons used to be so much better back then, you know? They actually had a storyline. Controlled? No, nobody is controlling my thoughts. I don't know why we keep going over this thought stuff. I'm telling you, I only came in because my husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm crazy. They're the ones doing things, not controlling my brain or whatever. They're just watching my moves at the warehouse.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like everyday events, like something you see or hear, are specifically related to you or have a hidden meaning that only you understand?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, like the truck I was telling you about. It's all connected. Everyday things are definitely part of it. Like I keep saying, I only came in today because my husband and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I'm the one who's crazy. But I know what's going on. They're watching my moves, changing the pallet counts so I don't get that supervisor spot. It's so obvious to me. \n\nSpeaking of obvious, you know what's not obvious? I tried to tighten the little knob thing under the sink, but I think I made it worse. I really should just call a plumber, but they charge an arm and a leg just to walk through the door. My neighbor, Gary, he said he'd look at it, but he's always busy working on his classic car in the driveway. It's a '67 Mustang, cherry red. Beautiful car. He spends every weekend waxing it. I used to want a convertible when I was a kid, so I could feel the wind in my hair... but anyway, yeah, the things I see at work, they definitely have a meaning that they think I don't see, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are not your own, or that your thoughts are being broadcasted for others to hear?\n\nPatient: Broadcasted? Like I've been saying this whole time, I only came in because my husband and the warehouse crew are acting like I'm the crazy one. But I know they're the ones watching my moves and messing with the pallet counts to keep me out of that supervisor spot. It's not my thoughts, it's my job they're messing with. \n\nIf I was supervisor, I'd get one of those heavy-duty walkie-talkies. The ones they give us on the floor now are basically toys. You have to press a little red button for an employee to come open it. I waited like ten minutes and nobody even came. The store was totally empty, too. Just me and this guy at the front buying a huge bag of dog food. It looked like the kind my aunt used to buy for her Golden Retriever. That dog shed everywhere. You couldn't sit on her living room couch without getting covered in blonde hair. She tried putting a blanket over it, but the dog would just pull it down... but anyway, no, my thoughts are my own. No, like a billboard? No. Look, I feel like a broken record here, but I keep telling you, it's not random stuff like that. It's the actual people in my life. Like I've said, I only came in here because my husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm crazy, but I know for a fact they've been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. It's not some secret message in a newspaper. 

I don't even read the newspaper anyway. My husband gets the Sunday paper, but only for the grocery coupons. He loves clipping coupons for the Food Lion down the street. They have this really good bakery section there. I usually get these blueberry muffins they make. They put the big sugar crystals on top, which gives it this nice crunch. I tried making muffins from a box mix a few weeks ago, but I left them in the oven too long and the bottoms got completely scorched. My oven runs really hot, I think. The dial is all rubbed off so you kind of just have to guess where 350 degrees is. I meant to buy an oven thermometer, but I keep forgetting every time I go to the hardware store. The hardware store always smells so good, though, like cut wood and fertilizer. It reminds me of my granddad's shed... but anyway, no, I don't think billboards or newspapers are secretly about me. It's just the warehouse crew pulling their stunts.

I know they've been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. It's so obvious if you just look at the numbers. Speaking of numbers, did you know I used to be really good at math back in high school? I mean, I didn't finish, but I was always the one figuring out the tips at restaurants. We used to go to this diner on 4th Street, they had the best cherry pie. I haven't had cherry pie in ages. My grandmother used to bake it from scratch, you know, with the lattice top and everything. She had this old rolling pin that was chipped on one end... anyway, yeah, they're messing with my pallet counts.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that others are intentionally trying to harm or undermine you at work, or that you have special abilities or insights others don't recognize?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, of course they are! Like I said, they're messing with the pallet counts on purpose. It's not just a mistake, they're intentionally changing the numbers so I look bad and don't get that supervisor job. My husband doesn't believe me, he just acts like I'm losing it, but I know what I'm seeing. I've always been really observant, you know? Like, I notice things other people just walk right past. I remember when I was a kid, I found this lost dog wandering around our neighborhood. Nobody else even saw him behind the bushes, but I did. He was a little terrier mix, scruffy looking thing. We kept him for a week before the owners put up flyers. We named him Buster. Buster loved eating hot dogs... but yeah, I definitely have insights they don't. I can see exactly what they're trying to do to me at the warehouse.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things in your environment, like objects or events, have special personal meanings or messages directed specifically at you?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, sometimes I do. Like the other day, I saw this red truck parked outside the warehouse, right by the loading dock. It was parked exactly in the spot where I usually take my smoke break. And it wasn't just any truck, it had this dent on the bumper that looked exactly like a 'V'. And I knew, I just knew, it was a message that they're watching my every move. They're trying to intimidate me so I don't go for that supervisor spot. They know I'm onto them changing the pallet counts. Speaking of trucks, my uncle used to drive a big rig. He'd haul freight all the way from Texas to Chicago. He brought me back a snow globe once from a truck stop in Missouri. It had a little arch inside it, and when you shook it, the snow was gold instead of white. I kept it on my dresser for years until my cat knocked it off and it shattered everywhere. Man, that cat was a menace. He was an orange tabby, always getting into things. But anyway, yeah, the red truck... it's all part of how my husband and the people at work are trying to make me look crazy, but I know what's really going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that you have a special role, destiny, or importance that others don't recognize, or that events happening around you are specifically directed at you in a way that others don't understand?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know about destiny, but I definitely have a role at the warehouse that they don't want to admit. Like I was saying, my husband and the people at work, they just keep acting like I'm crazy. But I know for a fact they've been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts so I don't get that supervisor spot. It's all connected. Just like how everything is connected if you really pay attention. I used to watch this show on TV about detectives connecting the dots on these big boards with red string. I always thought I'd be good at that, you know? Being a detective. My cousin actually wanted to be a cop, but he failed the physical because he has flat feet. He ended up working at a hardware store instead, mixing paint. I love the smell of fresh paint. We painted my bedroom a light blue when I was a teenager, like a sky blue. We got the paint on sale... but yeah, they're definitely targeting me with those pallet numbers because they know I'm the only one who actually understands how the warehouse should be run.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, taken out of your mind, or that others can hear what you're thinking?\n\nPatient: Put thoughts in my head? No, I don't... they don't need to do that. They don't need to hear what I'm thinking because they're already watching everything I do. Like I keep telling everyone, that's the whole reason I even came in here today. My husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm crazy, but I know they have been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. They're just doing it right out in the open on the floor. \n\nWe use these big wooden pallets mostly, the ones that are always splintering on the edges. You have to be careful or they'll rip right through your jeans. I ruined my favorite pair of Levi's that way last year. Got them caught on a stray nail on a busted pallet. They were the really comfortable kind, too, the bootcut ones. I used to wear them when I went line dancing with my sister. We'd go down to this place called The Rusty Spur on Friday nights. They had a mechanical bull, but I never rode it because I get motion sickness really easy. Even riding in the back seat of a car makes me dizzy. My mom used to have to give me Dramamine just to go to the grocery store when I was little... but anyway, no, nobody is taking my thoughts or anything. They're just watching my moves at the warehouse.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like everyday events, like something you see or hear, are specifically related to you or have a hidden meaning that only you understand?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, like the truck I was telling you about. It's all connected. Everyday things are definitely part of it. Like I keep saying, I only came in today because my husband and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I'm the one who's crazy. But I know what's going on. They're watching my moves, changing the pallet counts so I don't get that supervisor spot. It's so obvious to me. \n\nSpeaking of obvious, you know what's not obvious? How to fix a leaky faucet. My kitchen sink has been dripping for three weeks. Drip, drip, drip. It drives me up the wall when I'm trying to sleep. I tried to tighten the little knob thing under the sink, but I think I made it worse. I really should just call a plumber, but they charge an arm and a leg just to walk through the door. My neighbor, Gary, he said he'd look at it, but he's always busy working on his classic car in the driveway. It's a '67 Mustang, cherry red. Beautiful car. He spends every weekend waxing it. I used to want a convertible when I was a kid, so I could feel the wind in my hair... but anyway, yeah, the things I see at work, they definitely have a meaning that they think I don't see, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are not your own, or that your thoughts are being broadcasted for others to hear?\n\nPatient: Broadcasted? Like... like on a radio? No, I don't know why you keep asking about my thoughts. Like I've been saying this whole time, I only came in because my husband and the warehouse crew are acting like I'm the crazy one. But I know they're the ones watching my moves and messing with the pallet counts to keep me out of that supervisor spot. It's not my thoughts, it's my job they're messing with. \n\nIf I was supervisor, I'd get one of those heavy-duty walkie-talkies. The ones they give us on the floor now are basically toys. The batteries are always dying. I actually went to buy some double-As at the drugstore on Tuesday so I could just bring my own, but they were locked behind that thick plastic glass stuff. You have to press a little red button for an employee to come open it. I waited like ten minutes and nobody even came. The store was totally empty, too. Just me and this guy at the front buying a huge bag of dog food. It looked like the kind my aunt used to buy for her Golden Retriever. That dog shed everywhere. You couldn't sit on her living room couch without getting covered in blonde hair. She tried putting a blanket over it, but the dog would just pull it down... but anyway, no, my thoughts are my own. Nobody is broadcasting them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain things people say or do, even if not directed at you, actually have a special hidden meaning that only you understand?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess so. I mean, they think they're being sneaky with the things they do, but I see right through it. Like I keep telling you, it's the whole reason I'm even sitting here. My husband and the people at the warehouse are treating me like I'm the one losing my mind, but I know exactly what they're doing. They're watching every single move I make on the floor, and they're deliberately changing the pallet counts just to sabotage me so I don't get that supervisor position. \n\nIt's just like how they keep changing the schedule without telling me. The schedule is printed on this cheap paper, you know, the kind that jams the printer if you don't load it exactly right. We had a printer like that at my old job at the diner. It would always jam right when we needed to print the daily specials. The specials were usually meatloaf on Tuesdays and fried chicken on Thursdays. I always liked the fried chicken day because the cook, Hector, he made the best gravy. He used real bacon grease in it. It wasn't exactly healthy, but man, it tasted good. My doctor told me I need to watch my cholesterol, so I try not to eat stuff like that anymore. I've been trying to eat more oatmeal for breakfast, but it's just so bland unless you put a ton of brown sugar in it, which kind of defeats the purpose, I guess... but yeah, they think I don't know what they're doing with the pallets, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you sometimes feel that random or ordinary things — like something on TV, a song playing in the background, or something someone says in passing — are actually sending you a message or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know about TV or songs, really. It's more about the things happening right in front of me. Like I've been saying, I'm only here because my husband and the warehouse crew are trying to make it look like I'm crazy. But I know what's really going on. They're watching my moves, keeping track of everything I do, and changing the pallet counts so I don't get that supervisor spot. \n\nSpeaking of the warehouse, they finally fixed the vending machine in the break room. It was broken for like a month. It just kept eating my quarters. I lost probably three dollars trying to get a bag of pretzels. I love those little twisty pretzels, especially the ones with the extra salt. I used to buy the huge tubs of them at the wholesale club when my kids were little. They would eat them by the handful while watching cartoons on Saturday mornings. Cartoons used to be so much better back then, you know? They actually had a storyline. Now it's just flashing lights and loud noises. It gives me a headache if I watch it for too long. I usually just take an aspirin and go lay down in a dark room when I get a headache like that. But anyway, yeah, I don't really get messages from the TV or anything, it's just the warehouse people messing with my pallets.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being controlled or influenced by something outside of yourself, like a person, device, or force?\n\nPatient: Controlled? No, nobody is controlling my thoughts. I don't know why we keep going over this thought stuff. I'm telling you, I only came in because my husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm crazy. They're the ones doing things, not controlling my brain or whatever. I know they've been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. \n\nIt's just like how they handle the forklifts. You're supposed to sign them out on this clipboard by the manager's office, but half the time the pen is missing. It's one of those cheap ballpoint pens that always gets ink on your fingers. I got ink all over my favorite work shirt last week. It's a blue flannel one, really warm for when the loading dock doors are open in the winter. Winter has been so mild this year, though. We barely got any snow. I kind of miss the snow, to be honest. When I was a kid, we used to build these huge snow forts in the front yard. We'd stay out there until our toes were completely numb, and then my mom would make us hot cocoa with those tiny little marshmallows. I can never find the tiny ones at the grocery store anymore, just the big jumbo ones that don't melt right in the mug. You have to kind of push them down with a spoon, and then it just gets all sticky... but no, nobody is controlling my thoughts. It's just my husband and the crew messing with my job.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something completely unrelated to you — like something you saw on a billboard, a random conversation you overheard, or something written in a newspaper — was secretly about you or meant something special just for you?\n\nPatient: No, like a billboard? No. Look, I feel like a broken record here, but I keep telling you, it's not random stuff like that. It's the actual people in my life. Like I've said, I only came in here because my husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm crazy, but I know for a fact they've been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. It's not some secret message in a newspaper. \n\nI don't even read the newspaper anyway. My husband gets the Sunday paper, but only for the grocery coupons. He loves clipping coupons for the Food Lion down the street. They have this really good bakery section there. I usually get these blueberry muffins they make. They put the big sugar crystals on top, which gives it this nice crunch. I tried making muffins from a box mix a few weeks ago, but I left them in the oven too long and the bottoms got completely scorched. My oven runs really hot, I think. The dial is all rubbed off so you kind of just have to guess where 350 degrees is. I meant to buy an oven thermometer, but I keep forgetting every time I go to the hardware store. The hardware store always smells so good, though, like cut wood and fertilizer. It reminds me of my granddad's shed... but anyway, no, I don't think billboards or newspapers are secretly about me. It's just the warehouse crew pulling their stunts.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

A special mission? No, definitely not. I'm just a regular person trying to do my job. Like I keep saying, I just want that supervisor position, but my husband and the crew are doing everything they can to ruin it for me. We'd stay out there until our toes were completely numb, and then my mom would make us hot cocoa with those tiny little marshmallows. Powerful? No, I'm not powerful. I'm just a normal person. Look, I feel like I'm repeating myself over and over. I'm not some superhero or anything. I just want to do my job. Well, yeah, but not strangers. I feel like I'm just saying the same thing over and over to you. It's not random people, it's the guys at the warehouse and my husband. I just want them to stop messing with my counts so I can get my promotion.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that you are more important, capable, or powerful than others realize, especially in ways that are not obvious to the people around you?\n\nPatient: Powerful? No, I'm not powerful. I'm just a normal person. Look, I feel like I'm repeating myself over and over. I'm not some superhero or anything. I just want to do my job. Yeah, absolutely they are. It's been going on for way longer than a month. Probably since... I don't know, right around Memorial Day? Because I remember we had that barbecue, and my husband was texting on his phone the whole time instead of helping with the grill. I only like the spicy brown mustard, though. The bright yellow stuff is just too vinegary. My aunt used to make this potato salad with the yellow mustard and it was always so watery at the bottom of the bowl. She'd put celery in it too, which is just wrong. Celery doesn't belong in potato salad, it totally ruins the texture. You're chewing something soft and then suddenly there's a loud crunch. It's like finding a piece of eggshell in your scrambled eggs, you know? It just ruins your whole appetite. 

But yeah, I only came in here for this appointment because my husband and those warehouse guys keep acting like I'm the one who's crazy. But I know for a fact they are all in on it together. I keep my needles in one of those blue metal cookie tins, you know the ones? The Danish butter cookies.     "additional_requirements": [
      "Non-bizarre delusions present for at least 1 month",
      "No prominent hallucinations or other core symptoms of schizophrenia"
    ]
  }
}
